# Second opinion (Phase 4.6), M6 T33: EmulationStation's system layer, in pixelelated's EmulationStation fork

You are the one independent reviewer of a read-only renovation audit, read under a **new-functionality and brittleness lens**. Its subject is 77 files of `es-app/` at the EmulationStation commit `16dbac5211`, which the distribution pins:
- the game lists and their metadata (`Gamelist`, `MetaData`, `Genres`, `PlatformId`, `LangParser`, `FileTag`);
- the collections and filters (`CollectionSystemManager`, `FileFilterIndex`, `FileSorts`, `CustomFeatures`, `LibretroRatio`);
- the fork's 17 helper units (`AppWindow.h`, `CaptureRotation`, `CloudOffer`, `DisplayAspect`, `FolderMerge.h`, `JourneyTiers.h`, `RunLock.h`, `TextFit.h`, `WifiText`, `OfflineAchievementsText`, and others);
- `NetworkThread`;
- the surfaces outside the fork's lanes (`SystemScreenSaver`, `SystemRandomPlaylist`, `ContentInstaller`, `ThreadedBluetooth`, `ZaparooSupport`, `KeyboardMapping`, `components/ScraperSearchComponent`);
- the Windows and console residue.

**Provenance.** 27 files were added by the fork, and 4 were modified by it (`CMakeLists.txt`, `NetworkThread.cpp`/`.h`, `Win32ApiSystem.cpp`). 46 are byte-identical to ROCKNIX's master: they are read for brittleness, and a defect in one is still closed when its fix is contained.

**What ships.** An immutable handheld OS that a person updates in place, keeping `/storage`.
- EmulationStation is its interface, at 640x480 on the smallest panel, run as root.
- The build defines `NDEBUG`, so rapidjson's assertions are compiled out.
- A player's `gamelist.xml` is game content that the cloud restores from outside the process while ES may be running (D-CLOUD-049).
- Every writer is asked for temp-and-rename (D-CLOUD-078).
- `Utils::FileSystem::exists` caches each answer for the session until a launch resets it. ES's own writes through `Utils::FileSystem` drop their path from that cache.

**What you get.**
- The first reader is another lab's model. You get its `02-forward-audit.md`, `03-retrospective.md` and `04-analysis.md` whole. `02`'s tables are generated by tools whose methods are stated beside them.
- Then context: the brief's questions and the register rows the analyses cite.
- Then the host harnesses' output (`stage-c/runs.txt`), the floor read (`stage-c/floor.md`, made by a read-only subagent and labelled so), the include graph, the unit build, and the guest window's records.
- Then **the fork's own files, whole and line-numbered**: the 27 it added, plus `NetworkThread.cpp`/`.h`.
- Then **line-numbered excerpts of the upstream-carried files**, each cited range with eight lines of context. The rest is trimmed (D-WORKFLOW-195), and the manifest names every range sent.
- Then excerpts of the neighbours the leads turn on: `ViewController.cpp`, `FileData.cpp`, `SystemData.cpp`, `main.cpp`, `WatchersManager.cpp`, `FileSystemUtil.cpp`, `GuiGamelistOptions.cpp`, `GuiCollectionSystemsOptions.cpp`, `GuiMenu.cpp`, `GuiGamelistFilter.cpp`.
- **When a claim needs lines outside the packet, say `outside the packet` and do not guess.**

**Masking.** Every section passed through a mask that replaces a value after `key=`, `key:`, `token=`, `user=`, `pass...=` or `psk=` with `***`. A `***` is the mask, not the code. The packet was scanned for credential shapes before it left; no hit was a value.

**Your job is to refute, narrow or confirm, against the code: not to agree.** Cite `file:line` for every claim, and say plainly where the first reader is wrong. Its line numbers are the pin's.

**What was run.** Nothing can be run for you.
- Nine host harnesses compiled the pin's own code over doubles, under AddressSanitizer. Each failed at the pin and passed a constructed control (`stage-c/runs.txt`).
- The unit build ran every test target, `tests/app-unit` among them for the first time.
- A guest window ran an image whose EmulationStation is a later composition over the pin. It reproduced L-05 and L-28.

Answer these first, in this order:

1. **The game list's read and write (L-05, 3.13, 3.12, 3.14, 3.15).**
   - Is L-05 right that `Gamelist.cpp:413`'s cached `exists` makes `updateGamelist` start from an empty `<gameList>` and overwrite a restored file?
   - Is the miss really remembered before `:413` in a normal session? Name the earlier call that caches the gamelist path, or say none does. `loadGamelistFile` at `:112-117` uses `isRegularFile`.
   - Is `exists(path, false)` plus temp-and-rename enough? What does `packGamelist`/`cleanupGamelist` (`:518`, `:631`) do to a restored file?
2. **The lifetimes (L-06, L-07, L-12, L-15, L-24).**
   - Is L-06's free-then-read reachable, given that `:1302` makes the dynamic collection's root the bundle's child, from `GuiGamelistOptions.cpp:425` and `ISimpleGameListView.cpp:462`?
   - Are L-07's and L-12's proposed fixes sufficient? For L-07, consider `update()`'s join while the destructor also joins.
   - Is any other holder of a freed `FileData` in the slice missed?
3. **The parses (L-28, 3.21, L-27, L-17, and the rapidjson reads in `KeyboardMapping.cpp:236-292`).**
   - Under NDEBUG, which `GetString`/`GetArray` calls on a player-edited `.keys` file are wild reads?
4. **The bounds and the interface thread (L-01, L-02, L-04, L-09, L-11).**
   - Is L-01's chain real: does the interface thread wait on `mWatchersLock` while the watcher thread holds it across a network `check()`?
5. **The writes and the cache's third shape (L-23, L-03, `02` § The writes and § The reads).**
   - Is the claim right that ES's own `std::ofstream` and `pugi::save_file` writes leave the cache stale for `deleteCustomCollection` (`:1610`, `:1613`)?
6. **The words and the map (L-25, L-26, `02` § The words, `02` § The structure's proposed map text and the fourth check pattern).** Is any verdict or proposed text wrong?
7. **The floor (`stage-c/floor.md`).** Which subagent verdicts are wrong?

Then list, as **new items**, any defect the first reader missed. Give each its `file:line`, its family, and what would prove it. The families are:
- a check whose failure reads as success;
- a call with no bound;
- a lifetime or a thread;
- a read of a file another process writes;
- a write with no last good copy;
- a player sentence outside the vocabulary;
- a page the map does not describe.

**The shape of the answer.** One item per lead or finding, numbered `EP-1`, `EP-2`, ... Each item:
- names the lead (or `new`);
- gives a verdict: `confirmed`, `narrowed`, `refuted` or `new`;
- gives the evidence at `file:line`;
- says what the first reader should change, if anything.

Close with the items you would rank highest, and why.

## The library

One row per unit of the game lists, collections, metadata and filters. The facts are read at the cited lines, and the generator checks that each cited caller line holds the call it names. Three things stand out:
- **The first frame waits for the whole library:** every system's `gamelist.xml` parse, the filter index, the collections' load and es_features.cfg, under the splash. None of the slice's own reads is on the network.
- **Two writers are on the player's data:** `gamelist.xml` (game content, synced) and the collections' files (settings, archived). Both write in place (§ The writes).
- **One reader can lose data:** `Gamelist.cpp:413`'s cached read is the one that can drop a restored `gamelist.xml` (L-05).

## The helpers

One row per fork-added unit (17 units, 27 files). Its includers outside the 77 are the page or caller in another slice. The case file is matched by name.

Twelve units have a case file:
- Seven are built by `es-unit-tests`, which every lane has run.
- Five are `tests/app-unit`'s, which no lane has built (T18 `02:50-58`). Stage C's first build is theirs, extended in `t33-build-tests.sh`.

Five units have no case file:
- `CaptureRotation` and `DisplayAspect`: their pure halves are their `Text` siblings, which have cases.
- `CloudExit.h`: constants.
- `GameCubeRvzHashRetry.h`: 3.39 plans its case.
- `CloudOffer`: L-18's dialog, read.

The brief's "three without a case" counts `NetworkThread.*`, which is fork-modified, and leaves out the two `Text` pairs. The French is § The words': every msgid the 77 carry has a translation at the pin.

## The writes

§ 5's grep, line for line. Nine lines compile. The brief's "ten live sites" counts `CustomFeatures.cpp:151`, which is text inside a `/* */` block. `Win32ApiSystem.cpp:979` is dead inside `#if WIN32`.

None of the nine is temp-and-rename. `Gamelist.cpp:622` and `:841` keep a `.old` copy that nothing reads back. Every in-place writer is measured against two references:
- JourneyTiers' writer (`guis/GuiMenu.cpp:5066`, `AtomicFile::writeText`);
- es-core's `AtomicFileUtil`, which `es-file-tests` covers.

D-CLOUD-078 asks every writer for temp-and-rename. `Already written:` is answered per row (D-WORKFLOW-050).

## The reads

One row per `Utils::FileSystem::exists(` line: 83 lines and 86 calls. Four calls are uncached, all in `CaptureRotation.cpp` (`:113`, `:146`, `:161`, `:170`). The 46 upstream-carried files have none.

The cache's rule is T21's file, cited from T32's Q4 and not re-read:
- ES's own writes through `Utils::FileSystem` drop their path from the cache.
- A file written outside the process is not seen until a launch resets the cache (`FileSystemUtil.cpp:345`). Examples are the cloud's game-content restore (D-CLOUD-049) and the player over SSH.
- A third shape this slice adds: ES's own writes that do not go through `Utils::FileSystem`. These are `std::ofstream` (`CollectionSystemManager.cpp:345`) and `pugi::save_file` (`FileFilterIndex.cpp:1313`, `Gamelist.cpp:474`, `:622`, `:841`), and their paths are not dropped either.

The four the brief names, with their dispositions:
- **`Gamelist.cpp:413` (L-05):** the reader that can lose a player's data. The next save starts from an empty `<gameList>` and writes over the restored file.
- **`SystemScreenSaver.cpp:157` and `:164` (L-11):** recorded, outside the lanes.
- **`CaptureRotation.cpp:51`:** left cached on purpose, because it reads the image's read-only tree.

## The bounds

The table covers every network or script wait reachable from the 77, in three kinds: an HttpReq construction, a process start (§ 5's grep), and a wait the grep cannot see (L-01, L-02, L-09).

The brief's § 5 "HttpReq lines" (`OfflineAchievementsText.h:1`, `ScraperCmdLine.cpp:1`, `Win32ApiSystem.cpp:3`, `ZaparooSupport.cpp:5`) count every line naming HttpReq. The constructions among them are four: two live, both Zaparoo's, and two dead. The one option line is `ZaparooSupport.cpp:38`, a `connectTimeout`.

The slice's own code sets no total or stall bound anywhere. The one bounded process is `CloudOffer.cpp:37`'s `timeout 90` (L-18: the bound is sound). The unbounded live waits are:
- L-01: es-core's lock reached from the interface thread;
- L-09: Zaparoo's probe, its card and its lock, outside the lanes;
- `retroarch.cfg`'s read after every exit (L-02).

## The words

The 176 `_("` lines, 223 calls and 196 distinct msgids, each against the French catalog at the pin (`locale/lang/fr/LC_MESSAGES/emulationstation2.po`, continuation lines joined). Beyond the table, five more items:
- **L-25:** `FileTag.cpp:6-11` passes six of nine tag names through `fake_gettext_*`. `IN PROGRESS`, `DROPPED` and `ON HOLD` are not there and have no msgid; they reach T31's filter and editor pages in English under French. The catalog's six are at po 717, 3576, 3579, 3582, 3585 and 3588. proved: (stage C) 3.29's case; tool-run (`stage-a/runs.out`'s msgid grep).
- **L-26:** `LangParser.cpp:65`'s `"ìndia"` is a string literal, so xgettext is unaffected, but the token is unreachable. proved: (stage C) 3.31's case.
- **The dialog words** (7 `GuiMsgBox`, 1 `GuiLoading`, 2 text-edit popups; `stage-a/runs.out`):
  - `CloudOffer.cpp:51` has CHANGE FOLDER / CREATE ANYWAY / NOT NOW, and `:59` has CREATE IT / NOT NOW. Back falls through to the last button, NOT NOW, the safe one (`es-ui-style-guide.md` § Confirmations).
  - `:42`'s outcome box is DONE... / COULDN'T CREATE IT. CHECK YOUR CONNECTION AND TRY AGAIN FROM MANAGE CLOUD STORAGE. on rc alone (L-18).
  - `components/ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:386` is an English sentence-case message upper-cased by `toUpper`, outside `_()`, with a bare `"FINISH"`: no French reaches it.
  - `:468` reads AN ERROR OCCURRED: <the tool's error, upper-cased> with RETRY / SKIP.
  - `ThreadedBluetooth.cpp:54` and `:118` are single-OK boxes.
  - These four, the two scraper boxes and the two Bluetooth boxes, are upstream-carried and outside the lanes (D-UI-079), recorded.
- **L-10's toasts:** `ContentInstaller.cpp:144`, `:163`, `:182`, `:200`, `:218` and `:236` read `AN ERROR OCCURRED: <status>`, a tool's text on a player's toast. Recorded, outside the lanes.
- **The one help bar:** `ScraperSearchComponent::getHelpPrompts` (`:738-744`) adds ACCEPT RESULT to the grid's only while a row is selected. It offers a direction only where the grid moves (`es-code-traps.md` § The help bar).

Its choices are written to the collection's `.xcc` in place (`FileFilterIndex.cpp:1313`, L-23).

## Positives

- **The fork's helpers are pure where it matters.** Seven units with built cases (CloudFolderValidation 4, CheevosRetry 7, OfflineAchievementsText 15, CheevosIndex 6, WifiText 19, CaptureRotationText 8, DisplayAspectText 3; brief § 5's counts; the total is stage C's `TOTAL:` line) are string-in/string-out and fail closed (`CloudFolderValidation`'s `document()` refuses empty, oversized, NUL and an unknown schema).
- **CaptureRotation reads its four mutable inputs uncached and trusts its record only with `from=checked-launch`** (`:124`): a cut or foreign record reads as untrusted, the benign direction.
- **`AppWindow::post`'s two locks are taken in one order everywhere** (brief § 2, confirmed): the gate, then `Window::mNotificationMessagesLock`.
- **CloudOffer's dialogs put the safe choice last**, where back lands (NOT NOW), and its script carries its own bound (`timeout 90`).
- **Every msgid the 77 carry has French at the pin** (196 of 196). The three that FileTag never passes through `_()` are L-25, the exception that check cannot see.
- **No merge debt:** the 46 upstream-carried files are `rocknix/master`'s bytes.

## The first reader's 03-retrospective.md (whole)

# Retrospective audit — M6.P2 T33, EmulationStation's system layer (#680)

Read at the ES pin `16dbac5211` over the 77 paths of brief § 2: the game lists, the collections, the metadata and the filters; the fork's 17 helper units; the surfaces outside the lanes; and the Windows and console residue. The tables are `02`'s. The proofs are `stage-c/runs.txt` (nine harnesses, each FAIL at the pin and PASS under its control) and `02` § The guest cycle (the window, labelled `image`).

## 3.1 Architectural coherence

- **The fork's helpers are where the fork's rule is kept.** The pure `*Text` units, `CloudFolderValidation`, `CheevosIndex`, `CheevosRetry` and `WifiText` are string-in/string-out and fail closed, and seven of them have cases that every lane builds. The five header-only units (`AppWindow.h`, `FolderMerge.h`, `JourneyTiers.h`, `RunLock.h`, `TextFit.h`) had case files that no lane had built. Built at stage C, they pass, 18 of 18 (`stage-c/build-pin.txt`).
- **The fork wrote a rule and did not apply it everywhere.** `AppWindow::post` exists to drop a worker's post once the loop ends (F-ES-26). NetworkThread's five posts call `Window::postToUiThread` directly (L-12, `stage-c/l12-network-posts.py`). The rule holds in the files written after it, and not in the one written before it.
- **The library has two kinds of writer, and the cache knows only one.**
  - Through `Utils::FileSystem`, whose writes drop their path from the session cache (T21's file, cited).
  - Through `std::ofstream` and `pugi::save_file`, whose writes do not: `Gamelist.cpp:308`, `:474`, `:622`, `:841`; `FileFilterIndex.cpp:1313`; `CollectionSystemManager.cpp:345`.

  None of the nine compiled writes is temp-and-rename (`02` § The writes). The fork's own atomic writer (`AtomicFileUtil`, JourneyTiers' `AtomicFile::writeText`) is used by none of them.
- **A dynamic collection is the bundle's child, by identity.** `CollectionSystemManager.cpp:1302` adds the collection system's own root folder to the custom collections bundle. Any clear of that root deletes what the bundle's view points at, which is L-06's mechanism (3.5 below).

## 3.2 Project conformance

- **D-CLOUD-078 (temp-and-rename):** not met by any of the nine compiled writers. Two of them carry a player's data: `gamelist.xml` (game content, synced) and the collections' `.xcc` and `custom-<name>.cfg` (settings tier). 3.13 proves the gamelist write is in place (the same inode after the save).
- **D-CLOUD-049 (`gamelist.xml` is game content and is restored):** broken end to end by L-05. A `gamelist.xml` that arrives after the session's first look is read as absent and overwritten with the dirty entries alone (3.13 on the host; G-04 on the image, with its control).
- **D-UI-051 (every string ships in English and French):** met for the 196 msgids the 77 pass through `_()`. Three player words never pass through it: FileTag's IN PROGRESS, DROPPED and ON HOLD (L-25, 3.29).
- **`es-ui-style-guide.md` § Confirmations:** CloudOffer's two dialogs put NOT NOW last, where back lands. The scraper search's two upstream boxes do not use the fork's dialect: one is an English sentence through `toUpper` with a bare `"FINISH"`, and one carries a tool's error text (`02` § The words; outside the lanes).
- **D-UI-039 (the map):** two pages and one row are absent from `docs/es-menu-map.md`. `tools/es-menu-map-check` cannot see the pages, because their titles are passed in a member initializer (`02` § The structure). The coordinator recorded this as #951/#965's class.

## 3.3 Spec fidelity

The brief's figures, read at the pin, all hold (stage A; `02` § The runs). Where a reading sharpens or corrects the brief:
- **L-05:** the loss is total for the restored entries, not a stale read alone. `updateGamelist` starts from an empty `<gameList>` (`:427`).
- **L-06:** only a **dynamic** collection takes the repopulate path (`:1573`). G-02's plain custom collection could not show it.
- **L-28:** the mapping is dropped whole (`KeyboardMapping.cpp:292` keeps a mapping only with a trigger), not "an action with no trigger".
- **3.31:** the region is one string, so `usa, europe` reads region `us` alone.
- **`app-unit-tests`:** its CMakeLists puts no `fakes/` before `es-app/src` for this target (the brief said it did).
- **The bounds' "HttpReq lines":** these count every mention. The constructions are four, two of them live.

## 3.4 Platform conformance

- **Shipped build flags:** the recipe builds with NDEBUG (T32's finding), so the rapidjson reads in `KeyboardMapping.cpp`'s `load` (`:240-290`, `GetString` on any `type`, `mode`, `description` and `trigger` without `IsString`) are wild reads on a malformed `.keys` file in the shipped build, not assertions. This is recorded with L-28 for the stream; the harness proves only the dropped mapping.
- **Busybox and paths:** nothing in the slice shells out except `CloudOffer.cpp:37` (`timeout 90`, bounded) and the Windows residue.
- **The image:** the guest confirmed the paths the reads table assumes: `gamelist.xml` beside the ROMs, `recovery/<system>/` under the user's ES folder and emptied by a clean exit, `<rom>.keys` beside the game, and `/storage/.emulationstation` as a symlink to `.config/emulationstation`.

## 3.5 Cross-system interaction audit

### Interaction: the cloud's game-content restore x `Gamelist.cpp:413`'s cached read x the exit's `updateGamelist`
A restore writes `gamelist.xml` outside ES. The cache answers `:413` with the miss it remembered. The next save of any dirty game (a favourite, a play count) writes a `<gameList>` holding the dirty entries alone over the restored file. The player loses every scraped description and picture reference the restore brought. proved: 3.13 (host, FAIL then control PASS); G-04 (image, with a control system byte-identical).

### Interaction: EDIT DYNAMIC COLLECTION FILTERS x `reloadCollection(name, true)` x `ViewController::reloadGameListView`
From the bundle's view, with the cursor on a game in a dynamic collection, a filter change reloads the collection. `populateCustomCollection` clears the collection's root, which is the bundle's child (`:1302`). The reload then reads the freed cursor (`ViewController.cpp:1140-1142`), the #246 shape. proved: `stage-c/l06-reload-collection.py` (a heap-use-after-free under ASan at the pin; the control, reading the cursor before the clear, is clean). Not reproduced on the guest: G-02 drove a plain collection.

### Interaction: the scrape page's stop x `ScraperAsyncRequestsBuilder`'s thread x a slow token or hash
`stop()` and the destructor drop the builder while its thread runs `Scraper::search`. The class has no destructor and never joins or deletes its thread, so the thread writes into freed memory when the search returns. proved: `stage-c/l07-search-builder.py` (a heap-use-after-free at the pin; the control's destructor is clean).

### Interaction: `main()`'s end x `AppWindow::closing()` x NetworkThread's watchers
Between `closing()` (`main.cpp:1322`) and `WatchersManager::stop()` (`:1330`), a watcher's check can still post directly. Its task lands in a queue no loop will run, and `:266`'s token write is such a task. Sound by order, unsound by the fork's own rule. proved: `stage-c/l12-network-posts.py`.

### Interaction: a deleted custom collection x the session cache x the writers that bypass it
`deleteCustomCollection` (`:1610`, `:1613`) asks the cache for a file `std::ofstream` wrote this session. If an earlier read remembered the miss (`:963` reads the same `.xcc` path), the delete returns false and the file stays. Read, not run (`02` § The reads).

## 3.6 Time to play

- **Interface to a game's first frame:** nothing in the slice sits on the launch path, except `DisplayAspect`'s screenshot walk (L-04) on the first SCREENSHOTS look after a library change. On the guest, SCREENSHOTS changed 0.1 s after the press, over 100 captures and a four-game library. That does not price L-04 on a real library, which is the integration image's `time-to-play` cell.
- **A game's exit to the next first frame:** `CaptureRotation`'s read of `/var/log/exec.log` and `retroarch.cfg` after every RetroArch exit, on the interface thread (L-02). The log is bounded by the launcher's truncation; `retroarch.cfg` is bounded by nothing. Not measured here: the window launched no game.
- **The first frame:** waits for every system's `gamelist.xml` parse, the filter index and the collections (`02` § The library). The fixes proposed add one uncached `exists` per system at save time, not at startup.



## The first reader's 04-analysis.md (whole)

# Analysis — M6.P2 T33, EmulationStation's system layer (#680)

## Executive summary

- **What was read:** 77 paths of `es-app` at the pin `16dbac5211`: the game lists, collections, metadata and filters (46 upstream-carried, byte-identical to ROCKNIX's master); the fork's 17 helper units (27 files); and NetworkThread and the CMake list (4 fork-modified).
- **Proofs:**
  - Nine host fixtures, each extracting the pin's code as written, FAIL at the pin and PASS under the proposed fix (`stage-c/runs.txt`).
  - The unit build ran every target, `tests/app-unit`'s five case files for the first time (`TOTAL: 239 test cases in 5 binaries`, 0 failed).
  - One guest window reproduced L-05 and L-28 on the image (`02` § The guest cycle).
- **The highest finding is L-05,** in the lanes (D-CLOUD-049). A `gamelist.xml` restored from the cloud while ES runs is overwritten at the next save by the changed entries alone, because the read before the write is a cached miss (`SystemData.cpp:1653`, then `Gamelist.cpp:413`) and the write is in place (`:474`). Proved on the host (3.13) and on the image (G-04, with a control).
- **Two use-after-frees, proved under ASan:**
  - L-06: a dynamic collection's reload frees what the bundle view's cursor points at.
  - L-07: the scrape search's builder thread outlives its builder.
- **Fork rules the fork's own code misses:**
  - NetworkThread bypasses `AppWindow`'s closing gate (L-12).
  - TextFit's floor counts bytes (L-20).
  - None of the nine compiled writers is temp-and-rename (D-CLOUD-078).
- **Words:** three FileTag names a player reads have no French (L-25). One token is unreachable (L-26).
- **No merge debt.**

## Risk assessment

| Risk | Where | Who meets it | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A restored `gamelist.xml` loses its entries | L-05 | every player who restores game content from the cloud while ES runs, then plays or edits a game | **high** (data loss, in the lanes) |
| A dynamic collection's filter edit crashes ES | L-06 | a player who edits a dynamic collection's filters from the bundle with the cursor on one of its games | **high** (a crash; #246's shape) |
| A stopped scrape search writes into freed memory | L-07 | a player who backs out of a single-game scrape while its token or hash is being built | medium (a crash or heap corruption; scraping, outside the lanes) |
| Collections' files cut by a power loss | L-23 | settings tier, archived by the backup tool | medium |
| A refused trigger dropped silently; wild reads on a malformed `.keys` | L-28 | a player who edits a key map by hand | low (outside the lanes) |
| Tag names in English under French | L-25 | French players filtering by tag | low |
| A post lost at shutdown | L-12 | a token write racing the exit | low |
| An interface-thread wait on the watcher lock | L-01 | a page close during a network check | medium (a freeze bounded by the check) |

## Coverage boundary

- **Read whole:** the 27 fork-added files and NetworkThread. The upstream-carried files are read at the lines the leads, the floor and the generators cite. The floor's 35 non-harness rows were read by a read-only subagent (`stage-c/floor.md`, labelled), whose PRESENT verdicts the first reader re-read before carrying any into `05`.
- **Run:** the unit build (all targets), nine fixtures, and the guest window (EN).
- **Not run:** FR frames; the index toast; a launch, so L-02's `stat`; the content installer and NetworkThread's checks (no NIC, no account); the screensaver's video and slideshow modes; a TSan run (L-07's `mFinished`, L-12).
- **Outside the slice, cited:** `SystemData.cpp`, `ViewController.cpp`, `FileData.cpp`, `main.cpp`, es-core's `FileSystemUtil.cpp` and `WatchersManager.cpp` (T21's), and the pages (T31's).

## The questions

### Q1 Lifetimes and threads
- **L-06** (`CollectionSystemManager.cpp:1573-1600`, `:1302`, `:1115-1117`; `ViewController.cpp:1140-1142`): proved, a heap-use-after-free. The fix is es-code-traps.md:191's two halves: read the cursor path before the clear, and remake from it. Upstream-carried, contained (rule 6), with a #585 line.
- **L-07** (`components/ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:196-258`, `:206`, `:231`, `:358-361`): proved. The fix is a destructor that joins and deletes the thread, with `mFinished` atomic.
- **L-12** (`NetworkThread.cpp:206`, `:237`, `:251`, `:257`, `:266`; `main.cpp:1322`, `:1330`): proved by count and by the gate's behaviour. The fix routes the five posts through `AppWindow::post`.
- **L-15** (`FolderMerge.h:38-71` <- `SystemData.cpp:486`): its three cases pass. The holders' list is recorded for the rescan's owner.
- **L-24** (`NetworkThread.cpp:19-25`, `main.cpp:898`): sound for the process's life. Recorded so nobody frees the static.
- **L-10** (`ContentInstaller.cpp`, `ThreadedBluetooth.cpp`): `delete this` on the worker, the thread never joined. Outside the lanes, recorded, with a #585 line.

### Q2 Bounded calls
- **L-01:** the interface thread's `ResetComponent` (`NetworkThread.cpp:24`) takes `mWatchersLock`, which the watcher thread holds across every `check()` (`WatchersManager.cpp:181-196`). A page close waits out a network check. It is es-core's lock (T21's file): rule 6, put to the pause.
- **L-09:** Zaparoo's probe and card set no total or stall bound (`ZaparooSupport.cpp:39`, `:62`), and the lock (`:32`) inherits it. Outside the lanes, recorded.
- **L-18:** `timeout 90` is the bound, and it is sound (`02` § The bounds: 2 bounded of 7 live).
- **L-02:** `retroarch.cfg` (`CaptureRotation.cpp:162`) has no bound but its size, on the interface thread after every exit. The time-to-play line prices it.

### Q3 Reads that read as success
- **L-27** (3.9): `0.05` reads 0 STARS, and `3000…`/`0999…` read "" (`FileFilterIndex.cpp:331-339`, `:367`). Proved. Also, `1 STARS` has no singular.
- **L-28** (3.21): a refused trigger drops the whole mapping with no word (`KeyboardMapping.cpp:262-272`, `:292`). Proved on the host and the image. The loader's other reads (`:247-288`) call `GetString` with no `IsString`, which are wild reads under NDEBUG on a malformed file.
- **L-14:** `holder()`'s substring match (`RunLock.h:66-68`) would name an unrelated process whose argv contains `program`. Its three cases pass, but none covers the substring. A stream's case.
- **L-17:** `(int)v.GetInt64()` (`OfflineAchievementsText.cpp:18`). Harmless today.
- **L-18:** `CloudOffer.cpp:42-45` reads DONE on rc 0 alone. The script's half is the rclone slice's.
- **`MetaData::getInt`** (`:451`, `atoi`): reads 0 unchecked. Recorded.

### Q4 Uncached reads and in-place writes
- **L-05:**
  - **The read:** the first cached miss is `SystemData::getGamelistPath` (`SystemData.cpp:1653`), at the systems' load and again at `Gamelist.cpp:412`. Then `:413` reads the remembered miss.
  - **The write:** in place (`:474`).
  - **Fix:** an uncached read at `:413` and in `getGamelistPath` for the read path, and temp-and-rename at `:474`, `:622` and `:841`.
  - **`Already written:`** a `gamelist.xml` already cut by a past power loss is a parse error, and `:416-424` falls to an empty root. The fix reads both, so nothing is inherited beyond what a reread repairs.
- **L-23 and the third cache shape:** `std::ofstream` (`CollectionSystemManager.cpp:345`) and `pugi::save_file` (`FileFilterIndex.cpp:1313`) bypass the cache's invalidation. `deleteCustomCollection` (`:1610`, `:1613`) can refuse a file written this session.
- **L-03:** Backlog #1186's site.
- **L-13:** covered by `es-conf-tests`.

### Q5 The words, the French and the help bars
- **L-25** (3.29): IN PROGRESS, DROPPED and ON HOLD have no `_()` and no msgid (`FileTag.cpp:6-11` against `:16-18`). Proved. Proposed French, built as proposed (D-UI-112): EN COURS, ABANDONNÉ, EN PAUSE.
- **L-26** (3.31): `ìndia` (`LangParser.cpp:65`). Proved.
- **The upstream boxes:** `ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:386` (English, `toUpper`, a bare `"FINISH"`) and `:468`, and the installer's toasts (L-10). Outside the lanes, recorded.
- **The one help bar** (`ScraperSearchComponent.cpp:738-744`): sound.

### Q6 The views and their cost
- **L-04:** the first SCREENSHOTS look after a library change walks the library under `sShotLock`. On the guest it opened 0.1 s after the press over 100 captures and four games, which is not a price.
- **L-11 and `SystemRandomPlaylist`:** cached reads; outside the lanes, recorded.
- **The screensaver's launch** (`SystemScreenSaver.cpp:546`): it skips `ViewController::launch`'s format warning (`:497`) and BIOS check (`:507`), and keeps the cloud gate (`FileData.cpp:993`). Recorded (take-up row T2 `:293-294`).

### Q7 The structure, the map and the merge debt
- **The map:** the proposed text for FILTER GAMELIST BY, EDIT DYNAMIC COLLECTION FILTERS and UPDATE GAMELISTS is in `02` § The structure. The check's blindness to member-initializer titles is named with its fourth pattern. UPDATE GAMELISTS has two homes (GAME SETTINGS > TOOLS; GAME COLLECTION SETTINGS > OPTIONS), both seen on the guest for the first.
- **The residue:** L-21 and L-22, one row each.
- **The merge debt:** none.
- **Collisions:** T21's ten `CMakeLists.txt` lines and its `WifiText` delta, and T20's `OfflineAchievementsText` delta. A stream that edits these files names them.

## Renovation face

### Q1 Modernize
The writers want one atomic helper (`AtomicFileUtil`) where they now use `std::ofstream` and `pugi::save_file`. The collection reload wants the drop and remake halves #246 introduced.

### Q2 Standards
D-CLOUD-078 is met by none of the nine compiled writers. D-UI-051 is met but for three tag names. The fork's own `AppWindow` rule is not met by NetworkThread.

### Q3 Bugs
L-05, L-06, L-07, L-12, L-20, L-23, L-25, L-26, and L-14's substring. The proposed set and its owners are in `05`.

### Q4 Package currency
pugixml is a gitlink at the pin (`61c944895e…`). rapidjson is the recipe's 1.1.0. Neither is behind the tree's.

### Q5 Efficiency
L-02 (`retroarch.cfg` on every exit), L-04 (the screenshot walk) and L-01 (the lock wait). None was measured as dominating on the guest.

### Q6 Dead ends
`Win32ApiSystem.*`, the four Windows resource files, `ScraperCmdLine.*` (L-21, L-22), and `CustomFeatures.cpp:151`'s commented write.

### Q7 Clarity and structure
Seven headers absent from `ES_HEADERS`. `KeyboardMapping`'s loader, which never reports what it drops. The menu map check's three title patterns.

## Instruction File Recommendations

### Coverage gaps (would have prevented)
- `es-code-traps.md` § Pure text and § A rescan: nothing says that ES's own writes outside `Utils::FileSystem` leave the cache stale. The cached-read rule (`es-native-ui.md` § "A file another process writes is read uncached") covers other processes only, and L-23's third shape is the same process.

### Codification gaps (proposals for the coordinator)
- **`tools/es-menu-map-check` reads only three title shapes.** A fourth, `mMenu(window, _("..."))` in a member initializer, would see the two filter pages (#951/#965's class).
- **A writer rule a grep can audit:** `save_file(`, `std::ofstream` or `writeAllText(` on a player-data path, outside `AtomicFileUtil`.

## Second opinion

Filled after the two keyed calls (stage C's last step).

- **Every figure in `02` is a tool's line,** and every generator has a constructed negative.
- **Every proved lead names its artifact:** a guest step's file and frame, or a fixture's FAIL-then-PASS pair.
- **Void passes are recorded as void:** G-04's first read.
- **Corrections to the brief are listed** (`03` § 3.3).
- **What a subagent read is labelled** (`stage-c/floor.md`).
- **Not done:** FR frames; the index toast; L-02's `stat`; L-14's substring case; a TSan run.

## Context 2: the register rows

```text
| D-CLOUD-049 | 2026-09-06 | **The game list belongs to game content.** `gamelist.xml` is what the scraper writes beside the artwork, so it moves and counts with GAME CONTENT, not with ROMS AND BIOS: a ROMs-only backup neither uploads nor counts it, and a restored-then-scraped device reads IN YOUR CLOUD under ROMS AND BIOS alone. Favourites and play counts live in that file too; the class's description says GAME LISTS so that is not a surprise. Settles the question parked the same day; #61's "should backup upload a gamelist" is answered by the tick. Maintainer, 2026-09-06: *"I agree with you."* | #76, #77 |

| D-CLOUD-078 | 2026-09-10 | **Always keep a record of the last known good state. A failure reverts to it; a success becomes it, and the previous record is then removed (refines D-CLOUD-077).** Maintainer: *"we should always keep a record of the last known good state, which hopefully is a fairly straightforward but powerful rule. That way, if something fails, it should always revert to the last known good state. This also means we should always know when something is successful, and therefore the successful state becomes the last known good state, and we can remove the previous one."* Three obligations follow for every writer: (1) it must *know* when it succeeded -- a positive check of the artifact, never the absence of an error (engineering-practices § guards fail closed); (2) the new state replaces the old only after that check -- temp-and-rename, a marker written after the bytes are confirmed, a copy kept until the replacement is verified; (3) the superseded record is removed once, after the new one is good, so there is exactly one last-known-good at rest. Where it already holds: rclone's per-file rename, the settings-upload marker, `backuptool`'s rotation, the capture stage (content-addressed copies of every save the manifest claims), the device id's `.previous`, the `last-*` stamps. Where it does not yet: `system.cfg` and `es_settings.cfg` are rewritten in place and `chksysconfig` overwrites its `.backup` every boot with whatever is there -- so the RG SP's truncated file replaced its own good copy and defaults won (#102); a saves restore that overwrites a newer local copy keeps no previous version (`--backup-dir` would). Work: #105 (messages and retries), #104 (config files kept last-good and restored from it at boot instead of defaults), #22 (the reconciler's kept losers are this rule for saves). | #105, #104, #22, #102 |

| D-UI-039 | 2026-09-11 | **Settings do not stack in one window: a group that grows past a few rows nests behind a row of its own, and `docs/es-menu-map.md` is updated in the same change as every row added, moved or renamed.** Maintainer: "I don't want to start stacking too many settings in one window, so we may need to think about nesting some of what we're doing. This is one reason we should make sure we're updating our mapped mermaid or D2 file of everything we've done in EmulationStation." The map had last been touched 2026-09-09 and lagged five row changes; brought current the same day. | #134, #23 |

| D-UI-051 | 2026-09-13 | **A string the fork adds ships in English and French; other languages are added by whoever reads them. The mechanism is the one EmulationStation already has: every `_("")` string is keyed to `system.language` (SYSTEM SETTINGS > LANGUAGE), the build extracts the strings and merges them into each `locale/lang/<lang>/LC_MESSAGES/emulationstation2.po`, and a missing translation falls through to English. The French text is written into the fork's `.po` by hand, in that file's own style (accented capitals, the typographic apostrophe, the tabs and pages by their French names).** Maintainer, on #66: "is there a language setting on Rocknix that we can use to key the language to? I don't think we need to worry about supporting every language now, but we could at least support English and French, and other people could add other error messages for other languages if they choose." First applied to the thirteen scraper and RetroAchievements credential messages (ES `feature/scraper-login-words`); the rest of the fork's strings -- the cloud pages, FINISH RESTORE PROCESS and the others since 2026-08 -- have no French yet and are a follow-up. | #66, #68 |

| D-UI-079 | 2026-09-22 | **D-UI-078's foreground-page rule applies to the lanes the fork works in -- cloud sync and cloud behaviours, game saves, Wi-Fi, the offline achievements work -- and not to upstream surfaces outside them: the game index (`ThreadedHasher`), the content installer, the OS update and the Bluetooth scan keep their background cards for now.** Maintainer, 2026-09-22, closing #241's last box: *"We don't need to alter behaviors outside of the core work we've been tackling when it comes to the game index content installer, OS update, and Bluetooth scan. Let's not alter that work quite yet. I'm more comfortable modifying work within the different lanes we've been working in around cloud sync, cloud behaviors, game saves, Wi-Fi, etc."* The scraper was named in D-UI-078 itself and is done. | #241, D-UI-078 |

| D-WORKFLOW-050 | 2026-09-26 | **Every bug's code trace answers what the code before the fix had already written on devices and in their clouds, and how the fix treats it -- read both, migrated, or nothing inherited, argued -- as an `Already written:` line, and `tools/rc-preflight`'s `already written` item fails a candidate whose fixed bugs lack it (the clock from 2026-09-26 02:45 UTC).** Maintainer, after #288: *"shouldn't this be part of our general upgrade cycle and upgrade best practices? We should make sure that when we push a fix, we ensure parity for the existing system to leverage any new functionality. We can phrase this differently, but it should be part of our standard operating procedures and our instruction files, etc. This would have caught the need to rotate existing files if we'd followed this policy in place."* The rule existed (`upgrade-and-install.md`, July) and #280 broke it with a code trace, a VM proof and a staged candidate behind it, so it moves up a stage (`working-principles.md`: audited) rather than being reworded; a *migrated* answer adds its piece to `tools/vm-upgrade-rehearsal`'s seeds. | #289, #288 |

| D-UI-112 | 2026-09-28 | **The scan page and the top-up card say when achievement images could not be saved, and the offline summary says when a game's progress could not be read -- with proposed words pending the maintainer's approval:** scan page `SOME ACHIEVEMENT IMAGES COULDN'T BE SAVED. TRY THE SCAN AGAIN.`, top-up card `SOME ACHIEVEMENT IMAGES COULDN'T BE SAVED` (no instruction on that card, the F-RA-09 precedent), summary row `YOUR PROGRESS COULDN'T BE READ` with no bar (a null unlock count is unknown, not zero). Built with the proposed sentences rather than deferred (player-language.md § How to apply); the maintainer changes a word, not a build. | #307 PL-057, PL-060, #308 |

| D-WORKFLOW-164 | 2026-10-14 | **Consider upstream contributions for every confirmed fix or reusable improvement.** Extend D-WORKFLOW-162 beyond finding existing fixes: identify the component/ROCKNIX target, compare current upstream, and record a minimal independent patch with public regression evidence or an explicit duplicate/already-fixed/fork-specific disposition. Preserve licenses and separate dependency fixes from distro integration. Preparation does not imply submission or new publication authority. | #583; #574; `.claude/rules/engineering-practices.md`; `.claude/rules/fork-workflow.md` |

| D-WORKFLOW-165 | 2026-10-14 | **Keep a running upstream-contribution queue and submit after the initial build is complete.** The maintainer requests a durable list of planned upstream fixes, with outward submissions held until the initial build from the current M7 fix set and relevant qualification are complete. Preserve minimal patches/evidence during local repairs, but defer submission-only work and upstream-only reproductions instead of making them build prerequisites. Local defect repair and release checks remain required; build completion alone does not authorize posting. #585 is the live cross-project queue, retaining #168's detailed proxy map and explicit conditional/fork-only dispositions. | #585; #168; D-WORKFLOW-164; `docs/upstream/README.md`; `.claude/rules/engineering-practices.md` |

| D-WORKFLOW-195 | 2026-10-24 | **An Extended seat whose answer the model's output ceiling cuts on the packet is run again on a trimmed packet for that seat -- the same model, effort and provider; the analyses and tables whole, the cited files as line-numbered excerpts with context, the ask bounded -- never a model or effort substitution and never the same packet again.** `04` § Second opinion says the seats saw different evidence; an item one seat saw only in excerpt carries `excerpt`, and a disagreement that could be the excerpt's doing is resolved from the artifact, not from either seat. A fork resolved during execution on T30 (#677): Argon at `high` spent about 57k of its 65.5k output tokens reasoning on a 438 KB packet three times (`finish=length`), where T3's 308 KB packet took 30k. The maintainer may reverse it by a new row. | #677, #798; `adversarial-council.md` § Council model floor; the T30 hold post of 10:04 UTC and the coordinator's answer the same hour |

| D-WORKFLOW-209 | 2026-10-25 | **A renovation dead-end table removes nothing and edits no recipe: T23 (and T40, T43 and T25 after it) record a disposition per unit -- dead upstream-carried, shadowed copy with its override live or dead, unshipped, fork-own dead with a proposed removal and its `Already written:` line, or reached after all -- and the removal of upstream-carried dead code is one class decision for the maintainer, put to them on #346 with the table's counts and the cost against the value; a fork-own dead unit's removal is proposed on the punch list and left for the coordinator.** The 1,604 rows are 1,601 upstream-carried: the fork tracks `upstream/next`, a deletion of upstream's file is a modify/delete at every merge (D-WORKFLOW-198's class), and the area futro already held T7 to *deletes nothing upstream carries*. The order's § T23 said *decides the removals*; this row narrows it to *decides the dispositions*. The three IDs before this one are the M7 lane's reservation; this row is the first M6 row after it. | #670, #848, #346; `docs/reviews/briefs/generic-packages-dead-ends.md` § 1 |

| D-WORKFLOW-210 | 2026-10-26 | **A fork-only suite that asserts the defect a stream fixes changes in the stream's commit: exactly the checks the ruling reverses, each named with what it asserted before and after, each a recorded positive (FAIL on the unfixed script, PASS on the fixed), the whole suite passing after with its count named; no other line under `tools/`.** The second occurrence: T29's S3 changed `tools/vm-upgrade-rehearsal` (its seed held only while the first pass removed by name), and at T19's S1 and S3 `tools/last-good-scripts-test` asserted the no-credential offline token and the `0.0.0.0:8080` listener. Read with plan § 6b's stream practice (the suite before a covered script's commit): a suite that encodes the defect is otherwise a guard against the fix. The acceptance reads `git diff --stat <dispatch tip> <tip> -- tools` and expects exactly the suite with the named hunks. | #666 (the ruling of 14:33, `t19-streams.md` § The suite); #676 S3 (`t29-streams.md`); plan § 6b |

| D-WORKFLOW-211 | 2026-10-26 | **A suite fixture that relied on the defect a stream fixes is completed in the stream's commit: the setup line named, no assertion changed, beside the reversed checks D-WORKFLOW-210 allows.** At T19's S7 (a missing image helper is no longer a clean run) eleven of `tools/last-good-scripts-test`'s early scan and top-up cases failed because they ran with no image helper beside the ctl and the unfixed ctl read absence as clean; the fix is a stand-in helper at the section's setup, the helper every image ships. A fixture change that would alter what a check asserts is still a reversed check under 210, or a question. ## es-app/src/AppWindow.h @ 16dbac5211 (whole, 56 lines)
```

```text
    1  #pragma once
    2  #ifndef ES_APP_APP_WINDOW_H
    3  #define ES_APP_APP_WINDOW_H
    4  
    5  // A worker thread's post to the interface thread, and main()'s letting go of
    6  // the window (#308 8-es-menus-and-core claude F-ES-26).
    7  //
    8  // The window is a local of main(). Detached workers -- the network page's
    9  // address and SSID readers, the cloud hub's tidy-folders check, the exit's
   10  // capture, the offline achievements' cards -- post to it when their script
   11  // ends, and nothing joins them: one that ends after main() has torn the
   12  // window down posts into freed memory, a mutex and a vector in a dead
   13  // frame. So a worker posts through here, and main() calls closing() before
   14  // the teardown. The lock is held across the post, so closing() waits for
   15  // one in progress and no post begins after it: the check and the post are
   16  // one step, not two with a gap between.
   17  //
   18  // Templated on the window so the rule has a test without SDL.
   19  #include <functional>
   20  #include <mutex>
   21  
   22  namespace AppWindow
   23  {
   24  	inline std::mutex& gate()
   25  	{
   26  		static std::mutex m;
   27  		return m;
   28  	}
   29  
   30  	inline bool& open()
   31  	{
   32  		static bool isOpen = true;
   33  		return isOpen;
   34  	}
   35  
   36  	// Posts `task` unless the window is closing; false when it was not posted.
   37  	template <class W>
   38  	bool post(W* window, const std::function<void()>& task)
   39  	{
   40  		std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(gate());
   41  		if (!open() || window == nullptr)
   42  			return false;
   43  		window->postToUiThread(task);
   44  		return true;
   45  	}
   46  
   47  	// main(), once its loop has ended and before the window goes: every post
   48  	// from here on is dropped.
   49  	inline void closing()
   50  	{
   51  		std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(gate());
   52  		open() = false;
   53  	}
   54  }
   55  
   56  #endif // ES_APP_APP_WINDOW_H
```

```text
   32  	std::map<std::string, std::string> sTables;   // core -> table text
   33  
   34  	int fromTable(FileData* game)
   35  	{
   36  		FileData* source = game->getSourceFileData();
   37  		if (source == nullptr)
   38  			return 0;
   39  		const std::string core = source->getCore(true);
   40  		if (core.empty())
   41  			return 0;
   42  		std::string table;
   43  		{
   44  			std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lock(sLock);
   45  			auto it = sTables.find(core);
   46  			if (it != sTables.cend())
   47  				table = it->second;
   48  			else
   49  			{
   50  				const std::string path = std::string(TABLE_DIR) + "/" + core + ".txt";
   51  				if (Utils::FileSystem::exists(path))
   52  					table = Utils::FileSystem::readAllText(path);
   53  				sTables[core] = table;
   54  			}
   55  		}
   56  		if (table.empty())
   57  			return 0;
   58  		return CaptureRotationText::turnsFromTable(table, Utils::FileSystem::getStem(source->getPath()));
   59  	}
   60  }
   61  
   62  namespace CaptureRotation
   63  {
   64  	std::string recordPath(FileData* game)
   65  	{
   66  		if (game == nullptr)
   67  			return "";
   68  		FileData* source = game->getSourceFileData();
   69  		if (source == nullptr || source->getSystem() == nullptr)
   70  			return "";
   71  		for (auto rs : SaveStateConfigFile::getSaveStateConfigs(source->getSystem()))
   72  		{
   73  			const std::string dir = rs->getDirectory(source->getSystem());
   74  			if (dir.empty())
   75  				continue;
   76  			return dir + "/" + Utils::FileSystem::getStem(source->getPath()) + ".rotation";
   77  		}
   78  		return "";
   79  	}
   80  
   81  	// The turns read per record path, with the record's modification time
   82  	// when one existed (0 for a turn that came from the table). A record
   83  	// written behind the interface's back -- by hand over ssh, by a restore
   84  	// -- has a newer mtime than the one cached, and is read again; before
   85  	// this the cache was updated by recordAfterSession alone, so a record
   86  	// that arrived any other way was not seen until a restart (audit #258
   87  	// PL-019). The stat is one syscall a lookup; a screenshot list of a few
   88  	// hundred is a few hundred stats, once per rebuild of its tiles.
   89  	struct Known { int turns; time_t mtime; };
   90  	std::map<std::string, Known> sKnownAt;
   91  
   92  	static time_t recordMtime(const std::string& path)
   93  	{
   94  		struct stat st;
   95  		return ::stat(path.c_str(), &st) == 0 ? st.st_mtime : 0;
   96  	}
   97  
   98  	int read(FileData* game)
   99  	{
  100  		const std::string path = recordPath(game);
  101  		if (path.empty())
  102  			return 0;
  103  		const time_t mtime = recordMtime(path);
  104  		{
  105  			std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lock(sLock);
  106  			auto it = sKnownAt.find(path);
  107  			if (it != sKnownAt.cend() && it->second.mtime == mtime)
  108  				return it->second.turns;
  109  		}
  110  		// Uncached exists: the record is written while the interface runs.
  111  		int turns = 0;
  112  		std::string record;
  113  		if (mtime != 0 && Utils::FileSystem::exists(path, false))
  114  			record = Utils::FileSystem::readAllText(path);
  115  		// A record that does not say its turn came from the game's own
  116  		// launch was written by the reader that took the last rotation line
  117  		// of a log holding every launch since it was last removed (fork
  118  		// #280), and may carry another game's turn: the maintainer's Dr.
  119  		// Mario, F-Zero and Aladdin all read Ms. Pac-Man's 3 after the
  120  		// launcher was fixed, because nothing rewrites a record until the
  121  		// game exits again. So the core's table stands in for such a record
  122  		// until then (fork #288). Nothing is migrated and nothing removed:
  123  		// read both, write the new one.
  124  		if (!record.empty() && CaptureRotationText::recordFromOwnLaunch(record))
  125  			turns = CaptureRotationText::parseRecord(record);
  126  		else
  127  			turns = fromTable(game);
  128  		std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lock(sLock);
  129  		sKnownAt[path] = Known{ turns, mtime };
  130  		return turns;
  131  	}
  132  
  133  	void forgetAll()
  134  	{
  135  		std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lock(sLock);
  136  		sKnownAt.clear();
  137  	}
  138  
  139  	void recordAfterSession(FileData* game, const std::string& emulator, time_t started)
  140  	{
  141  		if (emulator != "retroarch")
  142  			return;
  143  		const std::string path = recordPath(game);
  144  		if (path.empty())
  145  			return;
  146  		if (!Utils::FileSystem::exists(LAUNCH_LOG, false))
  147  			return;
  148  		// A log older than the launch is not this session's: a launcher that
  149  		// did not truncate it left the previous game's section there (#308
  150  		// 8-es claude F-ES-08). Same clock, whole seconds: equal is this one.
  151  		const time_t logTime = recordMtime(LAUNCH_LOG);
  152  		if (logTime < started)
  153  		{
  154  			LOG(LogInfo) << "CaptureRotation: " << game->getName() << " -- the launch log predates this session; nothing recorded";
  155  			return;
  156  		}
  157  
  158  		const std::string log = Utils::FileSystem::readAllText(LAUNCH_LOG);
  159  		const int coreTurns = CaptureRotationText::turnsFromLog(log);
  160  		std::string config;
  161  		if (Utils::FileSystem::exists(RETROARCH_CONFIG, false))
  162  			config = Utils::FileSystem::readAllText(RETROARCH_CONFIG);
  163  		const int turns = CaptureRotationText::fold(coreTurns, config);
  164  
  165  		// The decision is CaptureRotationText's (a test reaches it): nothing
  166  		// from a log with no launch in it, a zero only where a record or the
  167  		// table would otherwise say something else, and an unchanged record
  168  		// left alone -- a record from before fork #288 is rewritten even when
  169  		// its turn agrees, so that it says where the turn came from.
  170  		const std::string record = Utils::FileSystem::exists(path, false) ? Utils::FileSystem::readAllText(path) : std::string();
  171  		if (!CaptureRotationText::shouldRecord(log, record, turns, fromTable(game)))
  172  			return;
  173  
  174  		Utils::FileSystem::createDirectory(Utils::FileSystem::getParent(path));
  175  		Utils::FileSystem::writeAllText(path, CaptureRotationText::recordText(turns));
  176  		LOG(LogInfo) << "CaptureRotation: " << game->getName() << " turns " << turns << " (core " << coreTurns << ") -> " << path;
  177  
  178  		{
  179  			std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lock(sLock);
  180  			sKnownAt[path] = Known{ turns, recordMtime(path) };
  181  		}
  182  		// The screenshots of this game were resolved to a Transform before
  183  		// the session; that cache is keyed by screenshot path and knows
  184  		// nothing of the record, so it is emptied here and rebuilt on the
  185  		// next look (audit #258 PL-019).
  186  		DisplayAspect::forgetScreenshots();
  187  	}
  188  }
```

```text
    8  class FileData;
    9  
   10  // A game's display rotation, learned at the end of each RetroArch session
   11  // from the launch log and kept beside its save states as <rom>.rotation
   12  // (fork #245, D-UI-081) -- so it travels with the states, and a game that
   13  // was never played here, which has no captures either, simply has none.
   14  namespace CaptureRotation
   15  {
   16  	// Quarter turns counter-clockwise, 0-3: the record when there is one
   17  	// and it says its turn came from the game's own launch (fork #288),
   18  	// else the core's own table (fork #248) for the game's ROM name, else 0.
   19  	int read(FileData* game);
   20  
   21  	// After a session: read the launch log and RetroArch's config, and
   22  	// write the record when it is new or has changed. Only RetroArch
   23  	// sessions write one; nothing else captures through the interface.
   24  	// `started` is when the launch began: a launch log older than that is
   25  	// not this session's, and nothing is read from it (#308 F-ES-08).
   26  	void recordAfterSession(FileData* game, const std::string& emulator, time_t started);
   27  
   28  	// Where the record lives, "" when the game's system keeps no states.
   29  	std::string recordPath(FileData* game);
   30  
   31  	// Forget every turn read so far, so the next read asks the record (or
   32  	// the table) again. Called when a record is written by something other
   33  	// than recordAfterSession -- the harness's synthetic line, a restore
   34  	// that brought records down -- and by the screenshot cache when a
   35  	// session has just recorded (audit #258 PL-019).
   36  	void forgetAll();
   37  }
   38  
   39  #endif // ES_APP_CAPTURE_ROTATION_H
```

```text
    1  #include "CaptureRotationText.h"
    2  
    3  #include <cstdlib>
    4  
    5  namespace
    6  {
    7  	// The record's second line since fork #288: the turn was read from a
    8  	// log that held this game's launch alone.
    9  	// The writer's claim about what it read. "from=own-launch" (fork #288)
   10  	// was written from a launch that failed and from a log with no launch
   11  	// in it, until the reading was checked (187ff9f1c, 190f62550): nothing
   12  	// can tell those records from good ones, so the claim this build
   13  	// trusts is a new one (audit of the fixes, E2 gpt G-E2-06).
   14  	const char* OWN_LAUNCH_LINE = "from=checked-launch";
   15  
   16  	// The value of `key = "value"` in a RetroArch config, or "" when absent.
   17  	std::string configValue(const std::string& config, const std::string& key)
   18  	{
   19  		size_t pos = 0;
   20  		while ((pos = config.find(key, pos)) != std::string::npos)
   21  		{
   22  			const bool atLineStart = pos == 0 || config[pos - 1] == '\n' || config[pos - 1] == '\r';
   23  			const size_t after = pos + key.size();
   24  			pos = after;
   25  			if (!atLineStart)
   26  				continue;
   27  			size_t p = after;
   28  			while (p < config.size() && (config[p] == ' ' || config[p] == '\t'))
   29  				p++;
   30  			if (p >= config.size() || config[p] != '=')
   31  				continue;
   32  			p++;
   33  			while (p < config.size() && (config[p] == ' ' || config[p] == '\t'))
   34  				p++;
   35  			if (p < config.size() && config[p] == '"')
   36  			{
   37  				const size_t end = config.find('"', p + 1);
   38  				return end == std::string::npos ? "" : config.substr(p + 1, end - p - 1);
   39  			}
   40  			const size_t end = config.find_first_of("\r\n", p);
   41  			return config.substr(p, end == std::string::npos ? std::string::npos : end - p);
   42  		}
   43  		return "";
   44  	}
   45  }
   46  
   47  namespace CaptureRotationText
   48  {
   49  	bool logHasLaunch(const std::string& launchLog)
   50  	{
   51  		return launchLog.find("=== Build ") != std::string::npos;
   52  	}
   53  
   54  	bool shouldRecord(const std::string& launchLog, const std::string& existingRecord, int turns, int tableTurns)
   55  	{
   56  		if (!logHasLaunch(launchLog))
   57  			return false;
   58  		if (existingRecord.empty())
   59  			return turns != 0 || tableTurns != 0;
   60  		return !(recordFromOwnLaunch(existingRecord) && parseRecord(existingRecord) == turns);
   61  	}
   62  
   63  	int turnsFromLog(const std::string& launchLog)
   64  	{
   65  		// Only the last launch's lines count (fork #280): the file held
   66  		// every launch since it was last removed on a device whose log level
   67  		// was none, and a vertical game's line at its end turned every game
   68  		// exited after it. RetroArch prints its build banner once per
   69  		// process, so the search starts at the last one; a log with none is
   70  		// no launch's. (Not the content line: a core that loads its own
   71  		// content -- fbneo, mame, the rotating ones -- gets "Content loading
   72  		// skipped" instead.)
   73  		const std::string banner = "=== Build ";
   74  		const size_t launch = launchLog.rfind(banner);
   75  		if (launch == std::string::npos)
   76  			return -1;
   77  		const std::string tag = "SET_ROTATION: \"";
   78  		size_t pos = launchLog.rfind(tag);
   79  		if (pos == std::string::npos || pos < launch)
   80  			return -1;
   81  		pos += tag.size();
   82  		if (pos >= launchLog.size() || launchLog[pos] < '0' || launchLog[pos] > '9')
   83  			return -1;
   84  		return (launchLog[pos] - '0') % 4;
   85  	}
   86  
   87  	int fold(int coreTurns, const std::string& retroarchConfig)
   88  	{
   89  		int turns = coreTurns < 0 ? 0 : coreTurns % 4;
   90  		const std::string allow = configValue(retroarchConfig, "video_allow_rotate");
   91  		if (allow == "false")
   92  			turns = 0;
   93  		const std::string own = configValue(retroarchConfig, "video_rotation");
   94  		if (!own.empty() && own[0] >= '0' && own[0] <= '9')
   95  			turns = (turns + (own[0] - '0')) % 4;
   96  		return turns;
   97  	}
   98  
   99  	int turnsFromTable(const std::string& table, const std::string& romName)
  100  	{
  101  		if (romName.empty())
  102  			return 0;
  103  		size_t pos = 0;
  104  		while (pos < table.size())
  105  		{
  106  			size_t end = table.find('\n', pos);
  107  			if (end == std::string::npos)
  108  				end = table.size();
  109  			const std::string line = table.substr(pos, end - pos);
  110  			pos = end + 1;
  111  			if (line.size() < romName.size() + 2 || line.compare(0, romName.size(), romName) != 0 || line[romName.size()] != ' ')
  112  				continue;
  113  			const char c = line[romName.size() + 1];
  114  			if (c < '0' || c > '3')
  115  				return 0;
  116  			return c - '0';
  117  		}
  118  		return 0;
  119  	}
  120  
  121  	std::string recordText(int turns)
  122  	{
  123  		turns = ((turns % 4) + 4) % 4;
  124  		return "turns=" + std::string(1, (char)('0' + turns)) + "\n" + OWN_LAUNCH_LINE + "\n";
  125  	}
  126  
  127  	bool recordFromOwnLaunch(const std::string& text)
  128  	{
  129  		// A line of its own, anywhere in the record, spaces and a carriage
  130  		// return around it allowed; never a substring of a longer line.
  131  		const std::string line(OWN_LAUNCH_LINE);
  132  		size_t pos = 0;
  133  		while (pos <= text.size())
  134  		{
  135  			size_t end = text.find('\n', pos);
  136  			if (end == std::string::npos)
  137  				end = text.size();
  138  			size_t from = pos, to = end;
  139  			while (from < to && (text[from] == ' ' || text[from] == '\t' || text[from] == '\r'))
  140  				from++;
  141  			while (to > from && (text[to - 1] == ' ' || text[to - 1] == '\t' || text[to - 1] == '\r'))
  142  				to--;
  143  			if (to - from == line.size() && text.compare(from, line.size(), line) == 0)
  144  				return true;
  145  			if (end >= text.size())
  146  				break;
  147  			pos = end + 1;
  148  		}
  149  		return false;
  150  	}
  151  
  152  	int parseRecord(const std::string& text)
  153  	{
  154  		size_t p = 0;
  155  		while (p < text.size() && (text[p] == ' ' || text[p] == '\t' || text[p] == '\r' || text[p] == '\n'))
  156  			p++;
  157  		const std::string key = "turns=";
  158  		if (text.compare(p, key.size(), key) == 0)
  159  			p += key.size();
  160  		if (p >= text.size() || text[p] < '0' || text[p] > '3')
  161  			return 0;
  162  		return text[p] - '0';
  163  	}
  164  }
```

```text
    1  #pragma once
    2  #ifndef ES_APP_CAPTURE_ROTATION_TEXT_H
    3  #define ES_APP_CAPTURE_ROTATION_TEXT_H
    4  
    5  #include <string>
    6  
    7  // How many quarter turns counter-clockwise RetroArch turned a game's frame
    8  // for the display, read from what it logged and configured (fork #245,
    9  // D-UI-081). The capture -- a save-state thumbnail, a screenshot -- is the
   10  // frame as the core drew it, so the interface turns it by the same amount.
   11  namespace CaptureRotationText
   12  {
   13  	// The last "[Environ] SET_ROTATION: "n" (deg)." line of a launch log
   14  	// AFTER its last "=== Build" banner (RetroArch prints one per process),
   15  	// as 0-3; -1 when that launch has none (a core that never asked), and
   16  	// -1 when the log has no banner at all. Only the last launch's section
   17  	// counts: the file held every launch since it was last removed on a
   18  	// device whose log level was none (fork #280), and a vertical game's
   19  	// line at its end turned every game exited after it.
   20  	int turnsFromLog(const std::string& launchLog);
   21  
   22  	// What the display did with the core's request: nothing when
   23  	// video_allow_rotate is off, plus the player's own video_rotation,
   24  	// mod 4. A core that asked for nothing counts as 0.
   25  	int fold(int coreTurns, const std::string& retroarchConfig);
   26  
   27  	// A core's table -- "<romname> <turns>" per line, generated from the
   28  	// core's own driver flags at build time (fork #248) -- read for one
   29  	// game: the turns, or 0 when the game is not in it.
   30  	int turnsFromTable(const std::string& table, const std::string& romName);
   31  
   32  	// The record's text -- "turns=N", then "from=checked-launch" -- and reading
   33  	// it back: N is 0-3 and anything else reads as 0. A bare digit reads
   34  	// too. The first line is longer than three bytes on purpose --
   35  	// readAllText skips a UTF-8 byte-order mark by reading three bytes
   36  	// first, and a shorter file comes back empty.
   37  	std::string recordText(int turns);
   38  	int parseRecord(const std::string& text);
   39  
   40  	// Whether the record says its turn was read from the game's own launch:
   41  	// a line "from=checked-launch" of its own. The reader before fork #288
   42  	// took the last rotation line of a log that held every launch since the
   43  	// file was last removed (fork #280), so a record it wrote may carry
   44  	// another game's turn -- Ms. Pac-Man's 3 on Dr. Mario -- and it wrote no
   45  	// such line. The builds after it wrote "from=own-launch", also over a
   46  	// launch that failed and a log with no launch in it (audit of the fixes,
   47  	// E2 gpt G-E2-06). A record without the checked line is not trusted: the
   48  	// core's table stands in until the game's next exit rewrites it. The
   49  	// line is the writer's claim about what it read, which is the one thing
   50  	// a migration could not know.
   51  	bool recordFromOwnLaunch(const std::string& text);
   52  
   53  	// Whether the launch log holds a launch at all: RetroArch's build banner.
   54  	bool logHasLaunch(const std::string& launchLog);
   55  
   56  	// Whether a session's reading is written as the game's record (#308
   57  	// 8-es claude F-ES-08, 8a gpt F-ES-08). `existingRecord` is "" when there
   58  	// is none; `turns` is what fold made of the log; `tableTurns` what the
   59  	// core's table would say without a record. Never from a log with no
   60  	// launch in it -- turnsFromLog's -1 there is "no evidence", which fold
   61  	// makes 0, and a record written from it said from=own-launch over a
   62  	// turn nobody read. A zero with no record is written only when the table
   63  	// would say otherwise: rotation turned off in RetroArch, a core that did
   64  	// not ask, and the table's 3 went on answering. Unchanged, never rewritten.
   65  	bool shouldRecord(const std::string& launchLog, const std::string& existingRecord, int turns, int tableTurns);
   66  }
   67  
   68  #endif // ES_APP_CAPTURE_ROTATION_TEXT_H
```

```text
    5  bool CheevosIndex::hasId(const std::string& cheevosId)
    6  {
    7  	const std::string id = Utils::String::trim(cheevosId);
    8  	if (id.empty() || id.size() > 12)
    9  		return false;
   10  	for (char c : id)
   11  		if (c < '0' || c > '9')
   12  			return false;
   13  	return Utils::String::toInteger(id) > 0;
   14  }
   15  
   16  CheevosIndex::Take CheevosIndex::take(bool forceAllGames, const std::string& cheevosHash, const std::string& cheevosId)
   17  {
   18  	if (forceAllGames || Utils::String::trim(cheevosHash).empty())
   19  		return Take::Hash;
   20  	if (hasId(cheevosId))
   21  		return Take::None;
   22  	return Take::Lookup;
   23  }
   24  
   25  bool CheevosIndex::lookupDue(long long lastEpoch, long long nowEpoch)
   26  {
   27  	if (lastEpoch <= 0 || nowEpoch < lastEpoch)
   28  		return true;
   29  	return nowEpoch - lastEpoch >= LookupIntervalSeconds;
   30  }
```

```text
    1  #pragma once
    2  #ifndef ES_APP_CHEEVOS_INDEX_H
    3  #define ES_APP_CHEEVOS_INDEX_H
    4  
    5  // What the RetroAchievements index (ThreadedHasher: INDEX NEW GAMES AT
    6  // STARTUP, INDEX GAMES) owes a game on a run, decided from the two fields it
    7  // keeps per game -- the ROM's cheevosHash and the game's cheevosId -- and
    8  // nothing else, so es-unit-tests can hold the rule (es-app/tests/unit).
    9  //
   10  // The rule used to be "a game with a hash is done". It is not: a game can
   11  // carry a hash and no id because the id was decided after the hash was saved
   12  // and never reached the disk (RC-5's recovery files, fork #183, audit #186
   13  // PL-08), or because RetroAchievements did not know the hash then. Such a
   14  // game gets a lookup -- its hash against the library the run has just
   15  // fetched -- and no file read; the ones the library knows gain their id,
   16  // the rest stay as they were. A game with an id is done, and a forced run
   17  // (INDEX GAMES with "all games") hashes everything.
   18  
   19  #include <string>
   20  
   21  namespace CheevosIndex
   22  {
   23  	enum class Take
   24  	{
   25  		None,     // the game has its id: nothing to do
   26  		Hash,     // read the ROM and look the hash up (the hash is missing, or the run is forced)
   27  		Lookup    // the hash is there and the id is not: look the hash up, read nothing
   28  	};
   29  
   30  	// Whether a cheevosId names a game: a whole number above zero, as
   31  	// FileData::hasCheevos reads it. "0", "", "abc" do not.
   32  	bool hasId(const std::string& cheevosId);
   33  
   34  	Take take(bool forceAllGames, const std::string& cheevosHash, const std::string& cheevosId);
   35  
   36  	// A lookup pass with nothing to hash beside it costs the hash library
   37  	// (two requests, several megabytes, on the interface thread at startup),
   38  	// and most libraries hold a game RetroAchievements does not know -- a
   39  	// hack, a bad dump -- whose hash would otherwise ask for it at every
   40  	// boot. So the silent startup run makes that pass at most once a day:
   41  	// due when none has run, when a day has passed, or when the clock has
   42  	// gone back. lastEpoch 0 is "never".
   43  	const long long LookupIntervalSeconds = 24 * 60 * 60;
   44  	bool lookupDue(long long lastEpoch, long long nowEpoch);
   45  }
   46  
   47  #endif // ES_APP_CHEEVOS_INDEX_H
```

```text
    1  #include "CheevosRetry.h"
    2  
    3  namespace CheevosRetry
    4  {
    5  	int nextDelayMs(bool unreachable, bool online, int unreachableInARow)
    6  	{
    7  		if (!unreachable || !online)
    8  			return ScheduledMs;
    9  
   10  		// The count is 1 on the first failure. Anything under that is a
   11  		// caller that did not count, and a guard that cannot see its input
   12  		// does not retry.
   13  		if (unreachableInARow < 1 || unreachableInARow > Attempts)
   14  			return ScheduledMs;
   15  
   16  		return IntervalMs;
   17  	}
   18  
   19  	// The save used to sign in whenever the switch was on and the token
   20  	// empty or the account changed: every close while offline was a
   21  	// request on the interface thread. Only a change the player made is
   22  	// answered here, and only when there is an address to send it from.
   23  	SaveSignIn saveSignIn(bool on, bool accountChanged, bool tokenEmpty, bool hasAddress)
   24  	{
   25  		if (!on)
   26  			return SaveSignIn::None;
   27  		if (accountChanged)
   28  			return hasAddress ? SaveSignIn::Now : SaveSignIn::Offline;
   29  		return tokenEmpty ? SaveSignIn::InBackground : SaveSignIn::None;
   30  	}
   31  }
```

## es-app/src/CheevosRetry.h @ 16dbac5211 (whole, 47 lines)

```text
    1  #pragma once
    2  
    3  // When the RetroAchievements token check tries again (#175).
    4  //
    5  // Pure: a schedule and nothing else, so es-app/tests/unit can hold it to
    6  // its word. The shell that runs the check and hears about the link is
    7  // CheckCheevosTokenComponent in NetworkThread.cpp.
    8  namespace CheevosRetry
    9  {
   10  	// A check that could not reach the server while the network is up is
   11  	// followed by another IntervalMs later, up to Attempts of them -- ninety
   12  	// seconds in all. A resolver that answers within a minute of the link is
   13  	// the common case (systemd-resolved lagged the address by five seconds
   14  	// on the run that found this); anything longer waits for the regular
   15  	// schedule, ScheduledMs.
   16  	const int IntervalMs  = 10 * 1000;
   17  	const int Attempts    = 9;
   18  	const int ScheduledMs = 120 * 60 * 1000;
   19  
   20  	// How long to wait before the next check.
   21  	//   unreachable:       the last check could not reach the server (a
   22  	//                      refusal and a success both reached it)
   23  	//   online:            the network watcher's view at that moment
   24  	//   unreachableInARow: checks since the last success, refusal or link-up
   25  	//                      that could not reach the server, the last one
   26  	//                      included -- so 1 on the first failure
   27  	// Anything but "unreachable, online, within the window" is the regular
   28  	// schedule: a refusal is the account's problem and no retry fixes it,
   29  	// and a device with no link has nothing to retry against.
   30  	int nextDelayMs(bool unreachable, bool online, int unreachableInARow);
   31  
   32  	// What closing RETROACHIEVEMENTS SETTINGS does about the token
   33  	// (#308 1-raoffline claude F-RA-19). The page's save runs on the
   34  	// interface thread, so a sign-in there is a request the screen waits on.
   35  	//   Now          sign in here: the player changed the switch or the
   36  	//                account, and is told how it went
   37  	//   Offline      the same change with no address to send from: no
   38  	//                request, the "couldn't reach" answer at once, and the
   39  	//                token check asked to run -- which, failing, retries
   40  	//                when the network comes up
   41  	//   InBackground nothing changed and there is no token:*** the token
   42  	//                check (NetworkThread, on its own thread) is asked to
   43  	//                run now instead
   44  	//   None         nothing to do
   45  	enum class SaveSignIn { None, Now, Offline, InBackground };
   46  	SaveSignIn saveSignIn(bool on, bool accountChanged, bool tokenEmpty, bool hasAddress);
   47  }
```

```text
    1  #pragma once
    2  
    3  // The exit codes the cloud scripts -- cloud_backup, cloud_restore,
    4  // cloud_content_backup, cloud_content_restore -- reserve for a run that did
    5  // nothing, and that EmulationStation names rather than reporting as FAILED.
    6  // The scripts hold the other half of this contract (take_cloud_lock and
    7  // check_network_link in each); the two sides change together and ship in
    8  // one image.
    9  //
   10  // Why these values. The sentinels were 3 and 4, which are also rclone's own
   11  // exit codes for "directory not found" and "file not found": a restore
   12  // against a cloud with no Saves folder yet exited 3 from rclone and the
   13  // transfer page said SKIPPED - ANOTHER CLOUD SYNC IS RUNNING over the files
   14  // it had just restored (fork #99). rclone's codes run 1-9, so the sentinels
   15  // come from sysexits.h instead, where nothing rclone returns can reach them.
   16  // 130 is what the scripts' trap exits with when somebody stops them, and
   17  // what a cancelled startup wait is reported as; it is here so every reader
   18  // spells the same thing the same way.
   19  namespace CloudExit
   20  {
   21  	constexpr int LockHeld  = 75;   // EX_TEMPFAIL: another cloud sync holds the flock
   22  	constexpr int NoNetwork = 69;   // EX_UNAVAILABLE: no default route, or no probe answered
   23  	// EX_CONFIG: the cloud has no saves folder to sync with yet. The scripts
   24  	// exit 0 and print ">>> offer create-saves-folder"; an automatic sync
   25  	// (startup, exit) records this code instead and says SKIPPED - YOUR
   26  	// CLOUD FOLDER ISN'T SET UP YET with the row to set it up on, rather
   27  	// than raising the question at startup (fork #353, D-CLOUD-166).
   28  	constexpr int NoFolder  = 78;
   29  	constexpr int Stopped   = 130;  // the scripts' SIGINT/SIGTERM trap; a cancelled run
   30  }
```
